Rule
(Gen-Inv)
.

The premises of the rule guarantee
for every sequence
\rho\in(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{\omega}
that
if
\langle\rho[0],\rho[1]\rangle\models\gamma
and for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
it holds that
\langle\rho[i],\rho[i+1]\rangle\models\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q)
,
then
\rho\models\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\alpha
.
Thus, the implication
\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q))\rightarrow\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\alpha)
is valid.
Therefore, the implication
\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{D}\rrparenthesis\rightarrow\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{D}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
is valid, and hence the rule is sound.

Rule
(Gen-Inv-P)
.

The premise of the rule guarantees that

\nu\models\alpha
if and only if
there exists a sequence
\rho\in(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{*}
such that

\langle\rho[0],\rho[1]\rangle\models\gamma
,
for every
i<|\rho|
it holds that
\langle\rho[i],\rho[i+1]\rangle\models\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q)
,
and for some
\nu=\rho[|\rho|-1]
.
Intuitively,
\alpha
characterizes precisely the set of assignments “reachable” from
\gamma
via a sequence of assignments in which every consecutive pair of assignments satisfies
\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q)
.
This means that
\alpha
is invariant on such sequences.
Thus, the implication

\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q))\rightarrow\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\gamma\rightarrow\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\alpha)

is valid.
Therefore, the implication
\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{D}\rrparenthesis\rightarrow\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{D}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
is valid,
and hence the rule is sound.

Rule
(Gen-Reach)
.

The premise of the rule guarantees that

\nu\models\gamma
if and only if
there exists a sequence
\rho\in(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{*}
such that

\rho[|\rho|]\models\beta
,
for every
i<|\rho|
it holds that
\langle\rho[i],\rho[i+1]\rangle\models\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q)
,
and
\nu=\rho[0]
.
Intuitively,
\gamma
characterizes a set of assignments from which every sequence of assignments in which every consecutive pair satisfies
\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q)
eventually “reaches’
\beta
.
Thus,
we have that the implication

\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\mathit{ImpInv}_{D}(q))\rightarrow\operatorname{\hbox to6.98pt{\vbox to6.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.11945pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -1.19 L 0 7.74 L 8.94 7.74 L 8.94 -1.19 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\gamma\rightarrow\operatorname{\hbox to8.7pt{\vbox to8.7pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25833pt\lower-1.76527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.51666pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 3.57 L 5.66 9.23 L 11.32 3.57 L 5.66 -2.09 Z}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\beta)
is valid.
Therefore, the implication

\llparenthesis E_{D}\rrparenthesis\wedge\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{D}\rrparenthesis\rightarrow\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{D}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis

is valid, and hence the rule is sound.

,

)
,

)
,
(Join-Imp)
.

All of these rules ensure that

\llparenthesis F_{D}\rrparenthesis\equiv\llparenthesis F_{D}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
and

\llparenthesis E_{D}\rrparenthesis\equiv\llparenthesis E_{D}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
for
D\in\{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{G}\}
, and that

\llparenthesis E_{\mathsf{A}}\rrparenthesis\land\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{A}}\rrparenthesis\equiv\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{A}}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
and

\llparenthesis E_{\mathsf{A}}\cup E_{\mathsf{G}}\rrparenthesis\land\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{G}}\rrparenthesis\equiv\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
.
This implies
\llparenthesis F_{\mathsf{G}}\cup E_{\mathsf{G}}\cup\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{G}}^{1}\rrparenthesis\equiv\llparenthesis F_{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}\cup E_{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}\cup\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}\rrparenthesis
as well as condition (b).
∎

Lemma A.7.

Let
M
be a monitor,
\Phi\in\mathit{RP{-}LTL}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
,
q\in Q
and
\pi\cdot\nu\cdot\rho\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{\omega}
for
\nu\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})
.
If
Equation

1
and
Equation

2
hold for
q\in Q

then they also hold on
\mathit{applyRules}(q)\in Q
.

Proof.

First note that
Definition

6.3
is closed under transitivity.
Hence,
Definition

6.3
also holds on
\mathit{applyRules}
.
To this end let
q=q_{1}
and
\mathit{applyRules}(q)=q_{2}
with the symbols of
Definition

6.3
.
To prove
Equation

2
it suffices to prove the stronger statement that
\llparenthesis E_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\rrparenthesis\to\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\rrparenthesis
and
\llparenthesis E_{\mathsf{A}}^{2}\cup E_{\mathsf{G}}^{2}\rrparenthesis\to\llparenthesis\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{G}}^{2}\rrparenthesis
are valid.
This follows from the precondition and
Definition

6.3
.
∎

Lemma A.8.

Let
M
be a monitor,
\Phi\in\mathit{RP{-}LTL}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
,
q\in Q
and
\pi\cdot\nu_{1}\cdot\rho\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{\omega}
for
\rho=\nu_{2}\cdot\rho^{\prime}
and
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})

If
Equation

1
and
Equation

2
hold for
q\in Q

then they also hold on
\mathit{nextState}(q,a)
for
a=\{p\in\mathcal{P}\mid\langle\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\rangle\models p\}

(with
\pi\cdot\nu_{1}
,
\nu_{2}
, and
\rho^{\prime}
as the respective trace elements).

Proof.

Equation

1
follows directly from
Lemma

A.3
and the precondition that the language equivalence holds on
q
:

\pi\cdot\nu_{1}\cdot(\nu_{2}\cdot\rho^{\prime})\models\Phi\iff\nu_{1}\cdot\nu_{2}\cdot\rho^{\prime}\models\mathit{Formula}(q)\iff\nu_{2}\cdot\rho^{\prime}\models\mathit{Formula}(\mathit{nextState}(q,a))

For
Equation

2
the expansion results shift in a similar fashion.
∎

Lemma A.9.

Let
M
be a monitor constructed from
\Phi\in\mathit{RP{-}LTL}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I}\cup\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
as described in
Section

6.1
,
Section

6.2
, and
Section

6.3
.
Then
M
satisfies the monitor-state correctness property (
1
) and the implied-state correctness property (
2
).

Proof.

We prove this statement by induction over the length of
\pi\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{*}
in both statements.
Note that we keep
\rho
and
q
in the inductive statement quantified.

Case

|\pi|=1
where
\pi=\epsilon\cdot\nu
for
\nu\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})
:
By definition,
\delta_{M}^{*}(\epsilon\cdot\nu)=q_{\mathit{init}}
.
As
\mathit{Formula}(q_{\mathit{init}})=\Phi
the monitor-state correctness property trivially holds.
Furthermore, as initially for
q_{\mathit{init}}
,
\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathit{Imp}_{\mathsf{G}}
are empty, the implied-state correctness property holds.

Case

|\pi|>1
where
\pi=\pi^{\prime}\cdot\nu_{1}\cdot\nu_{2}
for
\pi^{\prime}\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})^{*}
and
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})
:
Let
a:=\{p\in\mathcal{P}\mid\langle\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\rangle\models p\}
be then, by definition

\delta_{M}^{*}(\pi)=\delta(q^{\prime},a)=\mathit{applyRules}(\mathit{nextState}(q^{\prime},a))

where
q^{\prime}=\delta^{*}_{M}(\pi^{\prime}\cdot\nu_{1})
.
By induction hypothesis the monitor-state correctness property and implied-state correctness property hold from
q^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

A.8
and
Lemma

A.7
the also hold for
q
.
∎

Lemma A.10.

Let
M
be a monitor, such that the monitor-state correctness property (
1
) and the implied-state correctness property (
2
) hold for all
q\in Q
.
Then after applying the processing in
Section

6.4
, the monitor-state correctness property (
1
) sill holds for all
q\in Q
.

is indeed true on all traces where

.

See
6.11

Proof.

We already argue in
Section

6.5
why the constructed monitor is well-formed.

Lemma

A.9
and
Lemma

A.10
imply that the monitor-state correctness property holds for all states
q\in Q
of the monitor.
Hence, it remains to show that we assign the correct verdict according to
Definition

5.2
.

Let
\pi\in{(\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I}))}^{*}
,
\nu\in\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I})
, and let
q=\delta^{*}_{M}(\pi\cdot\nu)
.
Furthermore, let
\rho\in(\mathit{Assignments}(\mathbb{X}\cup\mathbb{I}))^{\omega}
.

If
\mathit{Verdict}(q)=\mathsf{UNSAT}
then
\mathit{Formula}(q)=\bot
, and hence,
\nu\cdot\rho\not\models\mathit{Formula}(q)
.
As the monitor-state correctness holds, we can conclude
\pi\cdot\nu\cdot\rho\not\models\Phi
, which proves the condition.

If
\mathit{Verdict}(q)=\mathsf{SAFETY}
and for
q_{i}:=\delta^{*}_{M}(\pi\cdot\nu\cdot\rho[0,i])
such that
q_{0}=q
and for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathit{Verdict}(q_{i})\neq\mathsf{UNSAT}
then by
Definition

5.1
and the definition of
\mathit{Verdict}
,
\mathit{Formula}(q_{i})
is syntactic safety and
\mathit{Formula}(q_{i})\neq\bot
.
Hence, expansion by
Lemma

A.3
implies that
\nu\cdot\rho[0,i]
is not a bad prefix of the safety language
\mathcal{L}(\mathit{Formula}(q))
.
Hence, by the property of a safety language,
\nu\cdot\rho\in\mathcal{L}(\mathit{Formula}(q))
.
As the monitor-state correctness holds, we can conclude
\pi\cdot\nu\cdot\rho\in\mathcal{L}(\phi)
which proves this case.

∎
